## 8. Complete card catalog

All 634 cards of the seven shipped sets — Core's 100 ([[§8.1]]–[[§8.5]]), Classic's 90 ([[§8.6]]), Classic+'s 78 ([[§8.7]]), Meditative's 102 ([[§8.8]], shipped with issue #553, [[R1420]]), Meditative+'s 26 ([[§8.9]], shipped with issue #1187 in v0.3.15, [[R1178000]]), Stinkyscape's 177 (175 until #2521) and its Dormant Starship ([[§8.10]], shipped with issue #1884, [[R1857000]], [[R1857001]]) and Training Grounds' 60 ([[§8.11]], shipped with issue #2204 in the v0.5 line, [[R2194000]], [[R2204000]]) — plus the tokens a card defines (Core's 5, index N.1, Classic+'s 28, Meditative's 30, Meditative+'s 7, Stinkyscape's 30 and Training Grounds' 1, index N.k, each after its card), the ten AI generated cards ([[§8.7]]'s end) and Stinkyscape's twenty-two shared tokens, its own ten AI generated cards among them ([[§8.10]]), all in index order (Core's 4 shared tokens, The Coin, the Ghoul Token and Classic's Glitch are in section 7), described by what they do to the game state rather than by their printed text; stats read base → radiant, and the Engine column names the primitive from section 6 or the ruling from section 11 that the implementation depends on. Every Radiant face meets [[R275]]'s standard, and `docs/radiant-audit.md` records each card against it. The catalog prints each Radiant face in full: its `radiant.text` is the Radiant cell read by the Conventions below and written out ([[R277]]). A card's `refs` list the cards and tokens its text names ([[R279]]).

Conventions: "target" means the player picks at play time from all legal units and heroes on either side unless narrowed. "Your" means the controller. A Cry or spell whose target set is empty fizzles: the unit still enters, the spell still counts as played. Damage always goes through the pipeline in section 4.4; "lose health" does not. Reading a Radiant cell: a cell that lists keywords without "Plus" gives the radiant form's complete keyword list; "Plus X" adds keyword X to the base keywords; a cell that names no keywords keeps the base keywords. A clause the cell restates replaces the base version and every base clause it does not restate is kept; a cell that changes only a number ("+10 attack", "5 damage") changes only that number. "same" says so explicitly, and "Also" and "Then" add effects.

Core's rarity is assigned by mechanical complexity and game impact, and is the value the catalog's `rarity` field carries. Common: keywords only, one primitive with at most one target, or one fixed start-of-turn, end-of-turn or cast-on-draw effect. Rare: a trigger that reads state or asks for a choice, a modal choice or prompt, a cost or mana modifier, or a simple trap. Epic: chained prompts, delayed or cross-turn effects, traps that act on the opponent's turn, or effects that read several zones. Legendary: control changes across the board, zone-wide rewrites, recursion, or a stat layer that exists for one card. Mythic: a subsystem of its own. Distribution: 32 Common, 40 Rare, 16 Epic, 7 Legendary, 5 Mythic (patch v0.2.9, issue #44, made #27, #28 and #40 Rare). This supersedes the rarities printed in the source list (#1–20 Common, #21–50 Rare, #51–80 Epic, #81–95 Legendary, #96–100 Mythic); a review comparing catalog to source should treat that difference as intended. Classic and Classic+ keep the designer's rarities, but for the twelve patch v0.2.9 moved ([[R739]]: C #3, #9, #12, #16, #18, #25, #28, #60, #61, #76, C+ #29 and #77): their source list gives rarity card by card, so each is a choice rather than an index block, and rarity feeds pools (a random Legendary, a random Book). Classic: 35 Common, 26 Rare, 18 Epic, 10 Legendary, 1 Mythic (90). Classic+: 13 Common, 24 Rare, 25 Epic, 13 Legendary, 3 Mythic (78). A Classic+ token carries `rarity: "Token"` like every token, so no pool ever finds one by rarity, and the rarity the designer printed on it as `printedRarity` ([[§5]]), which the Rarity column shows in parentheses: Legendary on the eight Pancakes, the five Losers, KY's Gift, Felinor Flagbearer Prime, Classic Golem and the J-lease Pack; Epic on Otherworldly Removal's three and Solarius Prime; Rare on Bone Storm and Brother Ping; Common, Common, Rare, Legendary and Mythic on the five Grapes; none on the AI generated cards.

Rarity measures complexity and how much a card can swing a game, not raw stats; power is tuned with numbers and Radiant. Common is one idea read in one pass: vanilla or keyword Units, or a single effect (damage, draw, heal, destroy, bounce, counter); it can touch one card in another zone or reset the board in one line, with no build-arounds and no rule changes. Rare is one idea with a twist: conditions, Choose one, cost changes, making cards Radiant, stealing a single card; small engines that repeat a simple effect; a theme rewarded that still works in any deck. Epic is engines and payoffs a deck is built around; repeated generation of a random card or token every turn; mass effects on the opponent's hand or deck; multi-part effects and set showcases. Legendary takes over, swaps or replaces a whole resource (deck, hand, board or Hero health); breaks a core rule for one player; named characters and effects a game is remembered by. Mythic breaks the frame of the game: sets rules for the whole match, persists across games, hands a turn to an AI, or could not exist on a normal card. Distribution targets: Common 20-40%, Rare 25-30%, Epic 20-30%, Legendary 10-15%, Mythic 5% or less.

Families share one rarity: 2+ cards with the same naming template, card type and effect shape, so one card can be guessed from another. The family's rarity wins over the criteria; a member too strong or weak for it changes numbers, not rarity (Book of Vital Kill stays Epic although setting Hero health is Legendary territory).

| Family | Rarity |
| --- | --- |
| Book of ___ | Epic |
| Call to Chaos (___ Edition) | Legendary |
| ___ Glowy Jelly Bean | Rare |
| The Power to ___ | Rare |
| ___ in the System | Epic |
| ___ of the Forgotten (Echoes, Curse) | Rare |
| Rapid ___ (Replenish, Draw) | Common |
| ___ Shroom | Epic |
| Plague Bringer (and Goliath) | Rare |
| Jlockeed ___ | Common |
| Right-house defender / Wrong-House Attacker | Common |

Tags are not families: Human, Felinor, KY, CN, Fruit and Plague cards take any rarity, and Call to Arms is no Call to Chaos, the templates differing. Every token's rarity is Token ([[§5]]); a token a Classic+ or a Meditative card defines, and M+ #16.1 (Epic) and M+ #20.1 (Rare), prints the rarity its designer gave it on its frame (`printedRarity`, `docs/classic-sets.md` B2.5, `docs/meditative-set.md` M2, [[R1178002]]), which no pool reads, while Core's tokens, the generic ones many cards make (Rush, Sheep, Felinor, Ghoul) among them, Glitch and the fifteen AI generated cards (C+'s ten and M+ #1.1 to #1.5) print none. Real cards made by other cards keep their own rarity (Book of Flame from Devil's Pact, Reminisce from Field of Dreams). A bigger version of the same effect is never a lower rarity than the smaller one (Call to Arms Recruits at Common, so Pile On's Recruit of the deck is Epic). Radiant never changes rarity. One name names one card ([[R739]]).

A Core row whose card declares numbers (`params`, [[R386]]) ends its Engine cell with "Tunes:", read as [[§8.6]]'s rows read it; a Core row without one declares none (patch v0.3.2, issue #493).

### 8.6 Classic

Ninety cards, `classic-001` to `classic-090`, from the designer's list of 2026-09-30 (patch v0.2.0, [[R380]] on). Classic generates only cards that exist elsewhere (the Felinor Token, C #16 Book of Flame, C #34 Ancient Acquisition), so it defines no token. The Engine column ends with "Tunes:", the numbers a Nerf, a Buff and C+ #41 KY's Constant may change (`params`, [[§5]], [[R386]]; a numbered keyword's number, such as C+ #19.1's Armor 3 or C+ #38's Spell Damage +2, is listed too, though the catalog keeps it on the keyword, where [[R386]]'s X change moves it, and not in `params`): ↑ means more is better for the card's controller, ↓ less; a step is 1 for a number up to 5, 2 for 6 to 12 and a quarter (rounded) above that, read off the base face's value (one step serves both faces), unless the cell names one; an amount never drops below 1, and a threshold moves toward harder for a Nerf. A number only the Radiant face's text prints is tuned on the Radiant face only: on the base face a Nerf or a Buff never draws it and it reads its printed value ([[R749]], [[R824000]]); one only the base face's text prints is tuned on the base face only ([[R1431]]). A number only the card's Cry reads (`cry` in `params`) is out of a Nerf's and a Buff's reach while the card is on the field, as its cost and its Tribute are: the Cry has resolved and the price is paid ([[R1748000]], [[R2261000]]); one only one face's Cry reads, the other face's lasting text reading it too, is out of reach there while the card shows that face ([[R2261001]]). Issue #1748 audited every Spell and Field Spell of every set: a number one prints is declared, unless it is a numbered keyword's, a price, an engine table's, a formula's term, the card's structure, a declared target count, one whose better way hangs on the target, an AI generated card's or an Immutable card's ([[R1748003]]).

| # | Name | Rarity | Cost | Type, tags | Stats | Base effect | Radiant effect | Engine |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Curse of the Forgotten Classic | Rare | 1 | Spell |  | Deal 1 damage to the enemy hero for each card in their exile; draw 1 | Also Recruit a card from their exile; if it is a Unit, it attacks the enemy hero at once | One hit of N on the enemy hero, N = the opponent's exile size as it resolves: "for each card" in one sentence is one hit, so Armor applies once, as Hearthstone reads it, and N = 0 is no hit ([[R63]]); then the draw. The Radiant face keeps the draw ("Also" adds to the base face, and [[R275]] wants it stronger). Recruit ([[§6.3]]) from the opponent's exile: exile is chronological ([[§3]]), so Recruit's top-down scan is newest first, and the most recently exiled permanent card there is summoned under your control, no Cry ([[R1]]), its owner unchanged, so it goes to its owner's piles when it leaves the field ([[§3.2]]); a Unit then makes one forced attack on the enemy hero ([[R53]]), summoning sickness ignored; with no permanent in their exile, or no open zone for the newest one, nothing is recruited (never an older card), and a recruited Trap is set face-down, read by you alone ([[R33]]). A `preview` ([[R280]]) shows N. Tunes: damage per card 1 ↑; draw 1 ↑. |

One Joro answers one targeting; a Spell that names several of your units redirects the first. Joro answers from the hand only, a Yu-Gi-Oh hand trap ([[R394]]). Its Radiant face doubles to 2/2 and adds Indestructible, an endless decoy. Tunes: none. |

With fewer than 2 other cards in hand it is not a legal target. It binds both players, its controller included. | 90 | In Too Deep | Mythic | 1 | Field Spell, Quickdraw, Quest |  | Indestructible; each quest you complete offers rewards, and the reward you choose sets your next quest (quest 1's text lives in the quest line, not the card text: it appears on the card face only after the card is played) | Each quest you complete gives every reward it offers, and you follow every path | Quests ([[§10.1]], [[R404]]): `memory.quest = { active, progress, done, auras }`, the tree data in the card file. Quest 1 opens as the card enters the field. Each quest, what completes it (counted from the moment it opens), and its rewards → next quest: 1, you have drawn 2 cards: A → 2, B → 3; 2, 2 enemy permanents have been destroyed by anything: C → 4, D → 5; 3, you control 4 or more permanents at once: D → 5, E → 6; 4, you Float 3 or more mana ([[§6.2]] Float): F → 7, G → 8; 5, your cards have dealt 15 damage to enemies in total: G → 8, H → 9; 6, your Units have 13 or more total attack and 13 or more total health at once: H → 9, I → 10; 7, you Float 5 or more mana: J, the end of the line; 8, 3 cards have entered either exile pile: K, the end; 9, a draw of yours takes the last card of your deck (a deck already empty when the quest opens completes it at once): L, the end; 10, your graveyard holds 6 or more Units: M, the end. Rewards: A, heal your hero 6; B, deal 3 damage to a target; C, return 2 random cards from your graveyard to your hand; D, place 3 Plague Counters ([[§6.3]], [[R689]]); E, a random Unit of yours gets +3/+3; F, bounce target permanent; G, your opponent discards 2 cards of their choice ([[R16]], [[R1279000]]); H, draw 2; I, Recruit a card; J, gain 100 mana; K, exile your opponent's deck; L, Aura: you may play cards from your graveyard (Play from the graveyard, [[§6.3]] Play); M, Aura: your Units have Indestructible. Completion is noticed at the state check after the event that completes it, on either player's turn, and the reward choice is a `reward` prompt ([[§10.6]]) for the card's controller then (a non-active player's prompt on the opponent's turn). Reward G's discard prompt is held by the opponent instead: while they are not active it waits for the start of their next turn, and the quest it leads to opens once the discard resolves, at once when their hand is empty, and never if In Too Deep left the field meanwhile ([[R174]], [[R1279000]]). The auras L and M hold while In Too Deep is on the field; it is Indestructible, so usually for the rest of the game, but a Tribute or an exile ends them, and leaving the field resets the quest line ([[R78]]). The view shows the open quest or quests with their progress ("1/2") and the rewards on offer; the collection prints the whole tree. Radiant: each completed quest grants all of its rewards and opens all of their quests, with no reward prompt; a quest reached by two paths opens once, and a reward two completed quests offer (D, G, H) is granted by each. The conditions read the designer's quests as countable ([[R404]]): "Destroy 2 cards" counts enemy permanents destroyed by anything, "Deal 15 damage" counts damage your cards deal to enemies, "Exile 3 cards" counts cards entering either exile, "Float" is unspent mana at the end of your turn, and "Draw your entire Deck" is done by the draw that empties it, never by cards leaving it any other way. Quickdraw: it starts in your opening hand ([[§2.1]]). Issue #1704 raised quest 3 from 3 permanents, quest 5 from 12 damage and quest 6 from totals of 10; the replays of v0.4.0f and before keep those goals ([[R767000]]). Tunes: none. |

### 8.7 Classic+

Seventy-eight cards, `classicplus-001` to `classicplus-078`, from the designer's list of 2026-09-30 (patch v0.2.0, [[R380]] on), each token a card defines right after it (index N.k, id `classicplus-NNN-k`), then the ten AI generated cards (T-AI-1 to T-AI-10, `classicplus-t-ai-01` to `-10`), which C+ #43 AI Slop and C+ #78 Claude's Datacenter hand out. The designer used five numbers twice and numbered two tokens as cards (one of them under a doubled number); the first card listed under a number keeps it, and the others are renumbered: Jogg's Box #47, Brother Lar #76, Brother Ping #76.1, Anti-Softlock #77, Claude's Datacenter #78, Felinor Flagbearer Prime #46.1 and the J-lease J-Jungle EX-plorer Pack #75.1 ([[§5.3]]). "Tunes:" is as in [[§8.6]].

It exiles every Unit on the field at once, the tops of piles only: a card beneath resumes ([[§3.2]]) and stays. The attacker is gone, so the attack is canceled and no combat happens ([[R44]]). The trap goes to the graveyard as it fires. Tunes: none. |

| 35 | Rollback | Legendary | 3 | Spell |  | Choose 1, 2 or 3. Return the board to how it was that many turns ago | Choose 1, 2 or 3, and your side, your opponent's side or both. Return that part of the board to how it was that many turns ago | Board snapshots ([[R419]]): at the start of every turn, before the mana refresh, the state records the field (both sides' unit piles and backrow zones as full instance data, the cards beneath a Stack included, and each side's Locks) and keeps the last four (`state.boardHistory`: this turn's and the three before). "N turns ago" is the snapshot taken at the start of the player-turn N before this one. N, and on the Radiant face the side, are declared at play ([[R81]]). Restoring a side takes three steps, each done on every restored side before the next: 1. each card on that side now that the snapshot doesn't hold leaves the field for its controller's hand (a Bounce, [[R747]]; [[R78]]'s reset; a full hand burns it, [[§2.4]]; a unit token ceases to exist, [[R11]]); 2. each card the snapshot holds is put back in its zone and place in its pile with the snapshot's instance data (damage, buffs, granted keywords, position, counters, memory, radiant flag, face-down or face-up, controller, and its definition, so a card transformed or fused since is restored as it was), moved from wherever it is now (a hand, a deck, a graveyard, exile, the other side) or recreated from the snapshot if it has ceased to exist (a token, a card transformed or fused away), and a card that goes back face-down into the backrow from anywhere else takes a fresh id as it goes ([[R227]]); 3. that side's Locks become the snapshot's. Nothing else changes: health, mana, decks and graveyards stay, bar the cards that moved, and any home-zone reservation of a moved card is cleared. The moves are not plays, summons or deaths, so no Cry and no Death: one `rolledBack` event names N and the sides, followed by each moved card's own event, hidden per zone ([[§10.8]]). `viewFor` never sends the history. Tunes: none. |

| 42 | KY's Test | Legendary | 1 | Spell, KY |  | Offer an easy, a medium, and a hard problem, each with a random reward. | Same, each with a random Radiant reward. | The question bank ([[R420]]). As the Spell resolves, one reward is rolled per difficulty (an entry of its list, uniformly; Hard has one), then the three difficulties are offered in a Discover ([[§6.3]]), each labeled with its rolled reward. Then an `answer` prompt, the multiple choice: a problem of the chosen difficulty (match rng), its statement and its four options in an rng-shuffled order; the answer is one option. The bank is data in `crates/cards`, public like the catalog, versioned with it ([[R388]]): entries `{ id, difficulty, statement, options (4), answer }`, at least 30 Medium and 30 Hard (`KY_TEST_MIN_PROBLEMS`); since issue #2179 the old Hard problems are the Medium ones and the Hard ones are new, at a doctoral level ([[R2179003]]). Easy problems are generated from the rng instead: a four-digit sum or difference, a and b drawn from 1000 to 9999 (`KY_TEST_FOUR_DIGIT_OPERANDS`), a difference written larger first, and three different wrong answers, each the answer moved by one of ±1, ±10, ±100 (`KY_TEST_FOUR_DIGIT_MISSES`); through v0.5.2 a + b from 10 to 99 and ±1, ±2, ±10 (`KY_TEST_EASY_ADDENDS`, `KY_TEST_EASY_MISSES`, [[R580]]), with the old bank, which `script_v0_5_2` keeps ([[R767000]]). Statements are plain text with Unicode maths (∫, ², √, subscripts), so a client needs no maths renderer. The engine checks the answer and the key never leaves it: it stays in the prompt's resume data (problem id or generated addends, option order, answer index), which `viewFor` never sends ([[§10.8]]); the chooser sees the reward labels, the statement and the options, the opponent only that a prompt is open ([[§10.6]]) and then the reward's ordinary events. A right answer gains the reward shown on that difficulty; a wrong one gives nothing, and the Spell was still played. Rewards: pools of non-token cards of every set ([[R380]]) but KY's Test ([[R387]]), repeats allowed ([[R60]]), the hand cap burning extras ([[§2.4]]); 3 The Coins are three Core The Coin tokens ([[§7]]), which need no pool ([[R244]]); the (2) and (4) Cost cards are priced per [[R65]]; the Legendary is any non-token Legendary set to (0) (`costOverride`), as the Medium (4) Cost cards are set to (1); "fill your hand" adds random Books until the hand holds 10; KY's Gift is C+ #42.1 at its printed (4) (patch v0.3.10; at `costOverride` 0 before). Patch v0.3.10 (#831) also made each Hard problem one step harder in its own topic. Radiant: every reward card is Radiant. The turn clock keeps running while a problem is open, as for every prompt ([[R79]]). The bank ships in the client bundle, so a determined player can look answers up; keeping the keys server-only would cost hotseat and practice the card. Tunes: none. |

The board is a grid of 20 cells from the caster's seat: x = lane − 1 on both sides ([[§3.1]] faces lane N with lane N), y = 0 the caster's backrow, 1 the caster's unit row, 2 the opponent's unit row, 3 the opponent's backrow. The player picks points, not coefficients: as the Spell resolves, one Discover at a time (a `mode` prompt, [[§6.3]], [[§10.6]]; patch v0.3.10, [[R831003]]) offers `PAPAYA_BASE_OFFERS` (2) cells, on the Radiant face `PAPAYA_OFFERS` (3), drawn from the match rng among the cells of every lane not yet used (empty, occupied or Locked alike), in lane order, until four are picked (`PAPAYA_MAX_CELLS`): there is no "done", and five lanes always leave the fourth Discover a lane ([[R1704000]]). The player's control over the curve so stays limited, and every answer is reachable by `legalActions`, the fuzz suite and the AI. (Before patch v0.3.10 each board-cell prompt offered every cell of the unused lanes; through v0.4.0f, issue #1704, it cost (1), both faces offered 3 cells, "done" could end the picks after the first, and the curve exiled its cards, which those versions' replays keep, [[R767000]].) The curve is the lowest-degree polynomial through the chosen cells (Lagrange interpolation in exact rationals, degree at most 3: one cell a constant, two a line, three a parabola, four a cubic). It is read at every lane, x = 0 to 4: a zone is on the curve when its cell satisfies y = p(x) exactly, so the curve also meets cells in lanes nobody picked (one cell's constant crosses its whole row, and a line through two cells may meet a third); between lanes it touches nothing. The card at each such cell is destroyed ([[R1704000]]): the top of each pile, face-down cards included, a card beneath resuming ([[§3.2]]); an Indestructible card stays, a Reborn Unit returns and Deaths fire. The Radiant destroys only the enemy's (rows 2 and 3). Before the destroys a public `curveDrawn` names the zones on the curve in lane order ([[R831003]]), which the client draws across the board; a client may show the equation the chosen cells make. The opponent sees that a prompt is open, then the curve and the destroys. Tunes: none. |

### 8.8 Meditative

One hundred and two cards, `meditative-001` to `meditative-102`, from the designer's list of 2026-10-08 (issue #496, `JackiOh_Meditative_Cards.md`) and the three cards the designer added after it (issue #571), each token a card defines right after it (index N.k, id `meditative-NNN-k`): thirty tokens, 132 rows. The designer used two numbers twice, listed one card twice and left one unnumbered; the first card listed under a number keeps it, and the others are renumbered: Shade-iris keeps #28 and its token Ancient Curse is #28.1, while Showdown becomes #98; Growing Felinor keeps #93 and its tokens #93.1 to #93.3, while Paranoia becomes #99; the unnumbered Small Time Recruits takes the hole at #62; the Empty Plot the designer numbered #100.1 is #97.1, the first of Jlockeed's Evil Blueprints' nine buildings (#97.1 to #97.9); and the Clip-Farming Lawyer listed twice, identically, is one card (#27). Greaser keeps #100; the designer's later #98 Gachaholic and #99 Catboy Maid SSR+ are #101 and #102. #98 to #102 close the table, in number order. The cards print Nerf and Buff, which players read for the engine's Degrade and Upgrade ([[§6.3]], [[R386]], [[R1320]]): the identifiers, the events and `tune_once` keep their names, as [[R373]] kept `library`; "Buff it twice" is two Upgrades, and "+2/+2" is a plain stat buff. A token carries `rarity: "Token"` and the designer's rarity as `printedRarity`, shown in parentheses, as in [[§8.7]]. The designer gave five cards no rarity, and the rubric above gives them one: Showdown, Dragon Fruit, Small Time Recruits and Jlarna Rare, House Party Common. Meditative: 29 Common, 29 Rare, 22 Epic, 16 Legendary, 6 Mythic (102). The set was built in the catalog before it shipped, batch by batch (BUILD M10), from the design brief `docs/meditative-set.md`, and ships with the last part of its patch (issue #553, [[R1420]]); its deck and trio code numbers are 3000 + n ([[R1410]]). In this section "M #N" is Meditative card N, and "#N", "C #N" and "C+ #N" are Core, Classic and Classic+ cards as elsewhere. A mechanic the engine lacks is named by its system in the brief (ME-TURN, ME-CN, ME-TRIG and the rest) and by the ruling the brief proposes for it (MD-A7 and the like), which takes an R number as the part that proves it lands. "Tunes:" is as in [[§8.6]].

| # | Name | Rarity | Cost | Type, tags | Stats | Base effect | Radiant effect | Engine |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Disruptive Disruptor | Common | 2 | Field Spell |  | Aura: you can't be forced to discard cards during your opponent's turn. Cry: draw 1 | Cry: draw 2 and gain 2 mana | NEW: a discard guard, a pure read beside [[R457]]'s draw limit, asked of the cards acting on the field: while the active player is not the guarded player, every effect discard from that player's hand (random, chosen or a whole hand) passes over its cards, so none moves, nothing answers it and no random number is drawn ([[R129]]), and a public `discardPrevented { player, count }` reports it (a new event with its M5-T4 and `SOUND_CUES` rows). A discard paid as a price, an Activate's cost ([[R384]]) or a targeting cost ([[R450]]), is not forced and still happens; Temporary ([[R637]]) discards on its holder's own turn, so the guard never meets it; several guards do no more than one; C #8 Pickle does not offer its discard option on a question asked while the guarded player is not active — its deferred questions open on their turn, where the guard sleeps ([[R800]], [[R1279000]]). It stops M #2's discard on the opponent's turn and M #8's Ascent 3. The Cry, when it is played or cast ([[R1]]), draws, and on the Radiant face then gains temporary mana; patch v0.3.10 (#831) gave the base face its Cry and the Radiant face its second card. Tunes: draw 1 ↑ (Radiant 2); mana 2 ↑, on the Radiant face only, which alone prints it ([[R749]], [[R824000]]). |
| 2 | Rampaging Rhino | Common | 2 | Unit | 5/9 → 10/18 | Rush, Windfury. Whenever this takes damage, discard a card | First Strike, Rush, Windfury; same | A trigger on a `damage` aimed at itself, as #91 Fed Fauci's: a hit that Armor or a cap absorbed, or a Divine Shield took, emits no `damage` and costs nothing ([[R63]]), and each hit of a sweep is its own instance ([[R59]]). Each one discards a random card ([[R682]]) from the hand of the Rhino's controller as the hit lands (once M #6 gives it away, the new controller's); an empty hand discards nothing; a hit that kills it discards nothing, the trigger it queued ending with that stay ([[R174]]); on the opponent's turn it is a forced discard, which M #1 stops ([[R780]], [[R800]]). The designer's Radiant face repeated the text with only bigger stats, and the brief gave it Trample; patch v0.3.10 (#831) gives both faces Rush and Windfury (it can hit twice the turn it lands, Units only, [[§6.1]]) and the Radiant face First Strike in place of Trample, at 10/18 ([[R275]]). Tunes: discards 1 ↓. |
| 3 | Jlockwork Machine | Rare | 3 | Unit | 10/10 → 20/20 | Rush. Whenever your opponent plays a card, exile the top 3 cards of your deck | Same | A trigger on the opponent's `cardPlayed` ([[§10.5]] step 4, before the card resolves; a cast's once the effect that cast it has resolved, [[R117]]): casts are plays ([[R70]]), a countered card was never played ([[§6.3]] Counter), and a Trap set face-down is a play. It exiles the top three cards of its controller's library, C+ #12.6 Frozen Wastes' pattern, fewer when fewer are left; an empty deck exiles nothing and deals no fatigue, an exile being no draw. Its name puns on Jlockeed, but it has no tag and is no "Jlockeed ___", so that family's rarity does not apply. Rush, the Radiant face's alone before, is on both faces since patch v0.3.10 (#831). Tunes: exiles 3 ↓. |
| 4 | Juicy Kumquat Melon | Epic | 2 | Spell, Fruit |  | Draw a (0), (1), (2), (3), (4) and (5) Cost Meditative card | Also they cost (2) less | Six draws in cost order: for each cost from 0 to 5, the topmost card of your library whose definition is of the Meditative set and whose cost is that number, read as [[R65]] reads it in the deck (an X card counts 0, an embiggen card its base price, its `costMod` included), drawn by name as a real [[§2.4]] draw (cast on draw, [[R58]]'s chain, the hand cap and [[R457]]'s limit all apply). A cost with no match draws nothing and deals no fatigue, and the scan draws no random number, the deck being shuffled already ([[R801]], which M #14 shares). The six cards are chosen as the effect begins and noted on the resolving card, so a cast-on-draw pause cannot lose them. The Radiant face gives each drawn card that reached the hand a `costMod` of −2. The Fruit tag, and its cost of (2), are patch v0.3.10's (#831). Tunes: discount 2 ↑, on the Radiant face only ([[R749]]). |
| 5 | Death by 1000 cuts | Rare | 1 | Spell |  | Echo X, Pierce: deal 1 damage to a Unit; X is 2 times your max mana | Plus Lifesteal; X is 4 times your max mana | NEW: a computed Echo X, a pure-read script hook that the printed Echo reads at [[§10.5]] step 4 as the repeats are queued: `echo` times the caster's max mana ([[§2.3]]: 1 on turn 1, 4 from turn 4), plus the card's Echo tuning ([[R386]]) and Twinspell's +1 ([[R30]]). X is fixed when the play is made, a random cast using its caster's max mana, and mana gained during the resolution changes nothing ([[R802]]). The declared target is any Unit on either side ([[R81]]); each repeat asks a fresh target prompt ([[§6.2]] Echo, [[§10.6]]) and fizzles with no Unit to hit. At 4 max mana that is 9 hits, 17 on the Radiant face. Lifesteal is printed on the Radiant face, as on #93.1 Combo-Fodder, and heals once per hit ([[§4.4]] step 8). Pierce, on both faces since patch v0.3.10 (#831), is printed on the Spell, so every hit ignores Armor ([[§6.1]], [[R346]]). Tunes: damage 1 ↑; echo 2 ↑ (Radiant 4). The text counts with `{echo\|time\|times}`. |

The declared target is a Unit you control, the top of its pile ([[R13]], [[R81]]); its cost is read before it moves, as it stands on the field ([[R65]]; an X Unit costs its X, [[R396]]; a token its printed cost), and the gain is temporary mana that may pass 4 ([[§2.3]]). With no free unit zone on their side the Unit stays and you gain nothing, the mana being the gift's price ([[R803]]); its owner does not change ([[§3.2]]), so a given Unit that dies goes to your graveyard. | 8 | Reach the Summit | Mythic | 0 | Spell, Quickdraw, Wincon |  | Costs (Ascent level), starting at 0; gain the effect of your Ascent level, then it goes up by 1; end of turn: return this to your hand. Ascent 0: heal your hero 2; 1: deal 2 damage; 2: draw 2; 3: your opponent discards 2 random cards; 4: exile 2 random enemy permanents; 5: add 2 random Radiant cards to your hand, they cost (0); 6 to 9: the climb (Reach for the stars, And when they're near…, Push even harder, And you might just…); 10: win the game | Costs (Ascent level − 1), at least (0); 3 of each | Quickdraw ([[R640]]); the Wincon tag is new. The level is [[R429]]'s play count: `counts_plays` counts each play at [[§10.5]] step 4, casts included (but not a cast on draw, [[R2179002]]) and countered plays never, and the count rides the card through every zone; while it resolves the level is the count less one, and a `preview` ([[R280]]) labeled "Ascent level" shows it. A copy starts at 0 ([[R57]]), an Echo repeat resolves the same level again, and a card made Radiant keeps its level ([[R840]]). A play gains only its own level's line, not every line up to it ([[R841]]). The cost is a computed cost, the level, or max(0, level − 1) on the Radiant face, with [[R65]]'s modifiers on top ([[R842]]). Ascent 1 targets any Unit or hero with a resolution prompt ([[§10.6]]), picked at random under a random cast ([[R843]]); Ascent 3 is a random discard ([[R682]]); Ascent 4 exiles different random enemy permanents ([[R60]]), tops of piles, face-down cards included and shown; Ascent 5 draws non-token cards of every set ([[R380]]) but this one ([[R387]]), Radiant with `costOverride` 0; lines 6 to 9 do nothing; Ascent 10 and above wins (ME-WIN, [[R850]]). The return is #31's end-of-turn hook, from the graveyard on the turn it was played ([[R153]], [[R155]]), a full hand burning it ([[§2.4]]); M #12 returns it mid-turn, so it can climb twice in a turn, and M #10 does not repeat it, a Spell's resolution being no Cry ([[R822]]). Ascent 10 costs (10) on the base face, beyond max mana, so it needs temporary mana (M #6, M #22's Greed, The Coin, #6 Mana Well). Mythic: it sets a rule for the whole match. Tunes: heal 2 ↑, damage 2 ↑, draw 2 ↑, discards 2 ↑, exiles 2 ↑, adds 2 ↑ (Radiant 3 each); the levels and their costs are the frame and are not tuned. |
| 9 | Joint Filing | Epic | 2 | Field Spell |  | Aura: your start-of-turn and end-of-turn effects trigger an additional time | Two additional times | ME-TRIG's turn-hook multiplier: as the start-of-turn and end-of-turn entries of [[§2.2]]'s trigger steps are queued ([[R62]], [[R68]]) for a player with an acting Joint Filing, each entry is queued 1 + N times in a row, each copy its own queued trigger re-read as it pops, so a card no longer in a zone that runs the hook fizzles ([[R153]]), with a state check between copies ([[R59]]) and fresh random numbers and prompts for each. It reaches field cards, the return-flagged Spells in your graveyard (M #8, M #21, #23, #31; [[R155]]) and "Animated on your turn" cards, and multiplies the End of turn effects M #12 triggers ([[R821]]); it does not reach C #62's start of the opponent's turn, delayed effects or the end-of-turn trap window ([[R62]]). Multipliers do not stack: the highest acting one holds, as Hearthstone's Drakkari Enchanter does ([[R820]]). Tunes: extra 1 ↑ (Radiant 2). |
| 10 | Double Counting | Epic | 2 | Field Spell |  | Aura: your Cry and Death effects trigger an additional time | Two additional times | ME-TRIG's Cry and Death multiplier, Hearthstone's Brann Bronzebeard and Baron Rivendare in one card. A Cry run at [[§10.5]] step 5 for a card you play or cast ([[R1]], [[R70]]), or one C #54 Rewind triggers ([[R467]]), runs N more times, each a whole effect list closed by its own state check ([[R59]]) and parked on `state.work` behind the first ([[R113]]), reusing the play's declared targets and modes (a target gone since fizzles that part, [[R174]]), a triggered Cry reusing its prompts' answers ([[R823]]). The extra count is read as the Cry first runs, and the extras end once the card has left its stay ([[R118]]); an extra run is parked on `state.work` only at a pause, never in advance, and with no multiplier acting the extra path is absent from the play. The Death hook ([[§4.5]] step 3) of a card that died under your control ([[R89]]) runs N more times on the same snapshot, in [[R68]]'s order; the count is read once the dying cards are off the field ([[R463]]). A Cry is a permanent's: a Unit's, a Field Spell's or an Animated card's labeled Cry, or a Field Spell's one-time text ([[R408]]); a Spell's resolution is none, though the engine runs it from the same hook, so Spells are not repeated ([[R822]]). Multipliers do not stack ([[R820]]). With M #12 it gives two (Radiant four) full rounds of End of turn effects. Tunes: extra 1 ↑ (Radiant 2). |
| 11 | Double Header | Legendary | 4 | Field Spell |  | The first card you play each turn adds a copy of it that isn't Radiant and costs (0) to your hand | A Radiant copy | A trigger on your `cardResolved` ([[§10.5]] step 7, as #33 Unstable Clone Machine's, [[R17]]) when the card heads the turn's plays ([[R213]]: "first" counts the player's plays, not a flag), adding a fresh card of the played card's definition (#39's form, [[R71]]), a fused or crafted card's transient definition included ([[R77]]), with `costOverride` 0, Radiant on the Radiant face; the definition comes from the event, so a card that has ceased to exist since (#41 Sheepish) still copies. Double Header's own play is the turn's first card, but it does not answer its own play ([[R119]]), so that turn copies nothing; a cast is a play and can be the first ([[R70]]); a countered card was never played, so the next card is the first; the copy is a second card and copies nothing ([[R826]]). A copy of M #8 starts at Ascent 0 (MD-A6). Tunes: setCost 0 ↓ (never below 0). |

End of turn: deal 1 damage to the enemy hero; then this damage permanently goes up by 1 | Armor 4; 2 damage, going up by 2 | At its controller's end of turn, one hit on the enemy hero through its Armor and caps ([[§4.4]]), then a `set_number` on its own `damage` param, C+ #41 KY's Constant's verb ([[R386]]'s `tuning.set`, `numberChanged`), to the damage plus the growth; the printed number shows the current value, so no `preview` is needed. "Permanently" is the card's own tuning: kept in every zone and through leaving the field ([[R78]]), carried by a copy ([[R57]]) and moved by a Nerf or Buff; it grows after the hit even when Armor took all of it, and every extra trigger (M #9, M #12) grows it ([[R825]]). "Your opponent" is the enemy hero. Tunes: damage 1 ↑ (Radiant 2, no maximum); growth 1 ↑ (Radiant 2); Armor 2 ↑ (Radiant 4). |
| 14 | Prime Time | Epic | 2 | Spell |  | Draw 3 cards with a prime index from your deck | Draw every card with a prime index from your deck | The index is the definition's catalog `index` ([[§5]]) read as a whole number: 2, 3, 5 … 101 are prime in any set, indices repeating across sets (#2 and M #2 both count); a token's N.k or T- index and a transient card's are never prime; the index is printed in the inspect overlay, and it is never a position in the deck, whose order is hidden ([[R804]]). Each draw takes the topmost match by name, a real [[§2.4]] draw with no random number ([[R801]]); the Radiant face draws every match in the library as it stood when the effect began, the hand cap burning the overflow. A deckbuilding payoff, so Epic: 25 indices under 100 are prime. Tunes: draws 3 ↑, on the base face only. |

It is a price for a play, so a cast pays nothing ([[R70]]). Tunes: surcharge 1 ↑ (Radiant 2). |
| 16 | Trenful Trickster | Rare | 2 | Unit | 1/4 → 2/8 | End of turn: summon a random Trap | Summon 2 random Traps | At its controller's end of turn, #67 Zoomerbin Oomen's random summon over the non-token Traps and Field Traps of every set ([[R380]]; a Field Trap counts as a Trap), set face-down into your leftmost open backrow zone ([[R64]], [[R688]]), the opponent seeing a back and its cost ([[R33]], [[R351]]); with no open zone nothing is summoned and no random number is drawn ([[R129]]). The Radiant face makes two picks, repeats allowed ([[R60]]): the designer's "a random Traps" is read as the plural, the Radiant face's change, since the stats alone would not meet [[R275]] (⚠ designer). 1/5 → 2/10 through v0.5.2 (issue #2179). Tunes: traps 1 ↑ (Radiant 2). |
| 17 | True Craft a Card | Mythic | 0 | Spell |  | Craft a card and add it to your hand; exile this | Craft a Radiant card | ME-CRAFT, a subsystem (`subsystems/craft.rs`). The Spell resolves into a `number` prompt for the crafted card's cost, 0 to `CRAFT_MAX_COST` (4), then a new `craft` prompt, a block editor in Scratch's manner whose options are up to `CRAFT_PRESETS` (4) complete recipes the engine rolls within the budget from the match rng (a Unit, then a Spell, a Field Spell and a Trap as the offer allows) and whose answer is a preset or any recipe of the player's own (MD-A30). Since #1563 the prompt offers, Kazakus's way, only four keywords, two hats and three effect verbs, drawn as it opens, and a recipe may use only those ([[R1563007]]); "Gain Armor" is no block ([[R1563009]]). The blocks are a card file's own pieces (type, stats, keywords, hooks and effect verbs), each priced in points (at most `CRAFT_POINTS_BASE` 2 + `CRAFT_POINTS_PER_MANA` 5 × the cost) and in lines of code (at most `CRAFT_LOC_BUDGET` 24, the card file's frame of 3 included), with at most `CRAFT_MAX_EFFECTS` 8 effects and each number 1 to `CRAFT_MAX_N` 10, every price a named constant. Only Cry and When cast take targeted blocks, each declaring its own play-time target ([[R81]], [[R883]]). An invalid recipe is refused with the state unchanged and the prompt still open. A valid one is compiled into a transient definition, as Fuse's are ([[R77]]): its id is always the recipe's digest ([[R179]], [[R468]], [[R882]]), and `CardDef.craft` keeps the recipe, so its script rebuilds in any process; it is of the Meditative set, not a token, Mythic, with no tags, its `loc` the counted lines, every number a param that Nerf and Buff reach ([[R386]]), and its Radiant face doubles every number and stat (`CRAFT_RADIANT_MULTIPLIER` 2) (MD-A32). It goes to your hand at its cost, Radiant on the Radiant face (Radiant being the flag, [[§5.2]]); a full hand burns it ([[§2.4]]). "Exile this" is the Spell's landing ([[§5.1]], [[R178]]). The AI and a timeout ([[R79]]) answer only with presets, `legal_actions` enumerating them ([[R90]], [[R881]]) (MD-A31). The client's editor shows the engine's own verdict through the WASM module, a points meter and a lines-of-code meter; the opponent sees a prompt open, then a card under the sentinel ([[R97]]), and its definition once a card of it is readable ([[R243]]). Distinct from #99 Craft a Card ([[R739]]). Built under [[R880]] to [[R883]]. The turn clock runs while crafting, as for C+ #42 KY's Test ([[R420]]) (⚠ designer). Tunes: none; the crafter's prices are config. |
| 18 | Expedition12 | Epic | 1 | Spell, Quickdraw |  | Lose (2) mana next turn; make a card in your hand Radiant | Plus Cleave | Quickdraw ([[R640]]). The loss is a next-turn rider ([[§6.3]] Mana, as #21 Hinder's): your next refresh gives 2 less, never below 0 ([[§2.3]]); an extra turn taken next is that turn; mana gained during it still adds. The pick is a declared hand pick ([[R81]], as C+ #41's) among your other non-Radiant hand cards, made Radiant ([[§5.2]]); with none the Spell still resolves ([[§8]]'s Conventions). The Radiant face's Cleave also makes the cards beside the pick in your hand Radiant, the one before it and the one after it ([[R1563001]]). Until #1563 it lost all mana next turn (ME-TURN's lost refresh, [[R844]]) and the Radiant face had Echo 1. Tunes: loss 2 ↓. |
| 19 | Expedition123 | Epic | 1 | Spell, Quickdraw |  | Lose (2) mana next turn, then (3) mana the turn after; add a Temporal Rift to your hand | A Radiant Temporal Rift | Expedition1234, losing three turns, until patch v0.3.10 (#831), and "Lose all mana for your next 2 turns" (ME-TURN's lost refreshes, [[R844]]) until #1563. Quickdraw ([[R640]]). Two next-turn riders ([[§6.3]] Mana, as M #18's): your next refresh gives 2 less, and a delayed step at the start of your next turn ([[§10.1]], [[R62]], after that turn's refresh) puts the 3 on the refresh after it, the number as it stood at the cast; each refresh never below 0 ([[§2.3]]). Then the Rift (M #19.1) into your hand, Radiant on the Radiant face; a full hand burns it ([[§2.4]]). An extra turn from the Rift is "next turn", as Hearthstone's Overload lands on Time Warp's extra turn. Tunes: loss 2 ↓, lossAfter 3 ↓. |
| 19.1 | Temporal Rift | Token (printed Epic) | 0 | Spell, Token |  | Take an extra turn after this one; you can take only one extra turn from Temporal Rift each game | Also gain 2 mana and 2 mana next turn; draw 2 | ME-TURN's extra turns: a new verb owes the caster one extra turn unless their once-a-game Rift flag is set, and sets it (public on the view); at the end of the owed player's turn the cleanup checks the turn cap, then starts that player's turn again in place of the opponent's, spending one. An extra turn is a whole [[§2.2]] turn: the turn count grows (max mana with it, and [[R2]]'s cap counts it), with its refresh, Brittle tick, start-of-turn triggers and draw; Units are no longer summoning sick; the gainer's "next turn" effects land on it and the opponent's wait for their next turn ([[R845]]). Gained on your own turn it follows at once; gained by a cast on the opponent's turn it follows your next turn; several are taken one by one ([[R846]]). The flag is set when a Rift grants a turn, either face, a copy or a stolen Rift; a later Rift resolves its other effects and grants none ([[R847]]). The Radiant face's riders are temporary mana, #24's next-turn rider and a draw. The client shows an "Extra turn" banner. It cost (2) until patch v0.3.13 (#1194). Tunes: mana 2 ↑, nextMana 2 ↑ and draw 2 ↑, on the Radiant face only ([[R749]]). |
| 20 | Aestheticize the Game | Mythic | 0 | Spell, Quickdraw, Wincon |  | Choose one; for the rest of the game you also win while it is true: your hero has 100 or more health; your graveyard holds 100 or more cards; your Units have 100 or more Attack and 100 or more Health in total | 90 each; then draw a card | Quickdraw ([[R640]]); the Wincon tag is new. ME-WIN's chosen condition: the three modes are declared ([[R81]]), and the chosen one installs a rest-of-game player effect on the caster, `altWin { condition, threshold }` (never expiring, [[R458]]), public on the view with its progress ("Health 47/100"). The state check reads it at its game-end point, where the hero check stands, each time the board settles ([[§4.5]]): the hero's health (no cap, [[§3]]); the count of your own graveyard (unit tokens never arrive there, [[R11]]); the sums of attack and of current health over your acting Units, tops of piles and animated cards, cards beneath a Stack not counted ([[R13]]), both at or above the threshold ([[R849]]). A condition lasts the rest of the game and cannot be removed; several kept conditions win on any one ([[R848]]). At that point a player whose hero is at 0 or less loses even if a win of theirs holds; otherwise a player meeting a win (a condition, or M #8's Ascent 10) wins (`GameOverReason::AltWin`, `WonByEffect`) and two winners draw; a win effect takes hold at the check closing its effect list ([[R59]]) ([[R850]]). The designer's Radiant face was only 10% easier; its draw is [[R275]]'s Spell rider (⚠ designer). The opponent never sees the choice in an event, view or log line until its reward or a judged Fortify Mind reveals it ([[R860]]). Fortify Mind (M #22.1) goes to the opponent's hand carrying the secret's id. The designer's Radiant face also copied Temporal Rift's note by mistake, and it is dropped; its rider is Fortify Mind's price, a tax the opponent pays or takes every penalty for, [[R275]]'s tangential rider (the designer's word stands). Handed out by C+ #23's pool ([[R382]]) it is played at its printed (10), with no Cry to fire. "Indestructable" is corrected. Tunes: none; the thresholds are ME-JADE's config. |
| 40 | Feng Shui | Legendary | 2 | Field Spell, CN |  | Aura: every card has an element (水, 木, 火, 金 or 土); auspicious behavior will be rewarded and inauspicious behavior will be punished; your last element and your opponent's last element are shown here. Aura: you have Luck 1 | Rewarded for you, and punished heavily for your opponent | ME-ELEMENT, a subsystem (`subsystems/feng_shui.rs`). A card's element is the Hetu (河图) element of its index's last digit: 1 and 6 水, 2 and 7 火, 3 and 8 木, 4 and 9 金, 5 and 0 土; an index with no digit is 土, and a fused or crafted card has its first ingredient's [[R980]]. Elements are no tags, read by no pool, filter or "shares a tag" rule [[R986]], and are shown on every readable card while a Feng Shui is on the field. Each player's last face-up play is recorded at [[§10.5]] step 4, casts included ([[R70]]) but not a cast on draw ([[R2179002]]); Traps, Field Traps and cards set face-down (Cloaked) are neither recorded nor judged, so the hidden rule never reveals a hidden card; a countered play keeps what step 3 gave it but is not recorded and takes no damage, and the record ignores [[R451]]'s AI-card skip [[R982]]; its two lines are `preview`s ([[R280]]) with the glyph as their display ([[R372]]). At step 3, where Gifted Program's flag is read ([[R213]], [[R214]], [[R449]]), each Feng Shui on the field judges the play from its player's last card: positive when the last element generates the new one (木→火→土→金→水→木), negative when it overcomes it (木→土→水→火→金→木), neutral otherwise and on a first play [[R981]]. A positive play becomes Radiant and resolves so; a negative one is given Brittle 2 there ([[R385]], [[R638]]) and, once it has resolved, takes a hit of 10 from Feng Shui on that player's hero through [[§4.4]] [[R983]]; Feng Shui does not judge its own play. The base face judges both players; the Radiant face rewards only its controller and punishes only the opponent, for 20; each Feng Shui judges on its own, so punishments stack [[R984]]. The hidden numbers are config (`FENG_SHUI_DAMAGE` 10, Radiant 20; `FENG_SHUI_BRITTLE` 2), printed nowhere and never tuned [[R985]]; 20 is the brief's reading of "heavily" (⚠ designer). A public `fengShui { instanceId, sourceId, player, outcome }` lets the client draw 吉 or 凶. Luck X (ME-LUCK) adds X to the Lucky of every roll with a "best" that its controller's cards make; a roll with no best is untouched ([[R32]]) [[R987]]. Tunes: luck 1 ↑ (never below 1). |

Prices are in yuan, by engine config: `YUAN_PER_COST` (10) times the printed cost out of play ([[R65]]) plus `YUAN_PER_RARITY` (5) times the rarity rank (Common 1 to Mythic 5; a token by its printed rarity, an AI card as Common), doubled for a Radiant card: a Rock is 10 ¥, a (1) AI card 15 ¥, a (2) Rare CN card 30 ¥. A barter exiles the hand card (public) and adds its price, doubled if it is Radiant, to the yuan; any number may be bartered ([[R1001]]). The AI answers it as any prompt, enumerating at most 6 lots, 10 barters and Leave and simulating each on determinized copies ([[R185]]); a timeout ([[R79]]) leaves ([[R1002]]) and the random policy answers uniformly ([[§10.7]]). | 71 | Pareto Optimality | Mythic | 3 | Field Spell |  | Cry: summon The Cane. Whenever your opponent plays a card that isn't the AI's optimal move, The Cane attacks a random enemy | A Radiant The Cane; whenever your opponent emotes or plays a card that isn't the AI's optimal move | The Cry summons M #71.1 into your leftmost open backrow zone ([[R64]]) and remembers its id. ME-PARETO (`subsystems/pareto.rs`) stays pure and never calls `crates/ai`: its judge is [[§10.7]]'s scorer. While a card with a new judging flag acts on your field, each `play` action the opponent makes is judged on the state before it, ahead of [[§10.5]] step 1: every card they could play now is scored from their own view (the dry-run base, whose stand-ins conceal your hand, both libraries and your face-down traps, [[R222]], and a per-instance dry run at the card's own price, tuning and face), and the play is optimal when its card's score equals the top score, ties sharing it; the verdict waits for the trigger on that play's `cardResolved` ([[R17]]); a countered play was never played, and casts ([[R70]]) are no decisions and are not judged (MD-D28). Only the Cane's attack makes it public, telling you that a better-scored card was in their hand; the judge's choice is shown to no one. Its cost is bounded (the hand cap times the scorer's dry-run plays) and paid only while a Pareto watches. The Radiant face also hears emotes (NEW: ME-EMOTE, revising [[R643]]): an `Emote` action, legal for either seat when no prompt or mulligan is open and only while the other seat controls an acting card that hears emotes, with a public `emoted` event its trigger answers; the actor relays an emote that passed [[R643]]'s gate as now and also mints it as that seat's `Emote` action through the reduce queue (logged and hashed), refusing one that arrives on the action channel; hotseat and practice send it when the view says emotes are heard, the AI persona's included ([[R645]]); the random policy and My Pawn never emote; every other emote stays [[R643]]'s cosmetic relay (MD-D29). The scorer rates Traps on printed data, so setting one is usually "not optimal", which is the judge's nature, not a special case. Tunes: none. |

### 8.9 Meditative+

Twenty-six cards, `meditativeplus-001` to `meditativeplus-026`, from the designer's list of 2026-10-18 (issue #1173, `JackiOh_MeditativePlus_Cards.md`), each token a card defines right after it (index N.k, id `meditativeplus-NNN-k`): seven tokens, 33 rows (four until #1563 added M+ #1.3 and #1.4, six until #2521 added M+ #1.5). The designer's numbers stand, but for the Radiant faces of #9 and #17, which patch v0.3.14 (#1220) lowered, as their rows say. Two tokens are the designer's, M+ #16.1 Jazakus Prime and M+ #20.1 Lil' Kenny, each carrying `rarity: "Token"` and the designer's rarity as `printedRarity`, shown in parentheses, as in [[§8.7]]; five are the AI generated cards M+ #1 Bob's Plan adds, M+ #1.1 Base Camp, M+ #1.2 Spa Day, M+ #1.3 Garage Sale, M+ #1.4 Leg Day and M+ #1.5 Star Chart (#1.3 and #1.4 since #1563, #1.5 since #2521), which print none, as the T-AI cards ([[R1178002]]). Classic #60 holds the name Pile On, so the designer's #17 Pile On is Dogpile ([[R381]], [[R1178002]]); #24, listed as "Unit, Spell", is a Unit; #26 Scratch Pad, given no rarity, is Mythic, as M #17 True Craft a Card. The designer's "Jlockheed" is the tag Jlockeed ([[R278]]), and the set adds one tag, Potion ([[§5]], [[R1178000]]). Meditative+: 5 Common, 9 Rare, 9 Epic, 1 Legendary, 2 Mythic (26). The set was held by [[R1420]]'s gate until the last part of its patch, which shipped it in v0.3.15 (issue #1187, [[R1178000]]); its deck and trio code numbers are 4000 + n ([[R1410]]). It was built in the catalog card by card, batch by batch (BUILD M11), from the design brief `docs/meditative-plus-set.md`, whose proposed rulings (MP-B01-1 and the like) took R numbers from each part's issue block as the part that proved them landed. Its cards are written in the four patches' terms that landed beside it: an embiggen card's dearer price is its Embiggened version, labeled "Embiggened (N): …" (#1175), Freeze is #1174's, and a card in Chinese is CN (#1176). In this section "M+ #N" is Meditative+ card N, "M #N" a Meditative card, and "#N", "C #N" and "C+ #N" are Core, Classic and Classic+ cards as elsewhere. "Tunes:" is as in [[§8.6]].

### 8.10 Stinkyscape

One hundred and seventy-seven cards, `stinkyscape-001` to `stinkyscape-177`, from the designer's list of 2026-10-25 (issue #1836, `JackiOh_Stinkyscape_Cards.md`), each token a card defines right after it (index N.k, id `stinkyscape-NNN-k`), thirty in all; then the set's shared entries, at T- indices (id `stinkyscape-t-<name>`): the Dormant Starship, the twelve shared tokens (Drone Interceptor, Drone Corvette, the Drone Battleship's three layers, the six Resources and Anomaly Found!) and the ten AI generated cards S T-AI-1 to S T-AI-10 (`stinkyscape-t-ai-01` to `-10`): 230 rows (228 until #2521 added #176 Preemptive Strike and #177 Cloaking Module). The designer used #21 and #122 twice; his second #21 is #174 Aerelret and his second #122 #175 Frost Module, and every other number stands ([[R1857001]]). A token carries `rarity: "Token"` and the designer's rarity as `printedRarity`, shown in parentheses, as in [[§8.7]]; the four War Doctrines, whose headers print no Token, are tokens, and the ten AI cards, which the build designs (`docs/stinkyscape-set.md` ST15), print none, as Classic+'s ten. The Dormant Starship is the set's one entry that is neither a numbered card nor a token: a (3) Legendary Unit, 0/0, that the engine summons when a Starship Piece dies ([[R1852000]]), its Radiant face [[R349]]'s fallback, in no pool and no deck unless a query names it ([[R1857001]]). The designer's types and tags stand, with the brief's fixes: #125 and #164 are Field Spells and #125 is Jlockeed, #91 and #92 are Units tagged Swarm, #40 is Plague, #16 Rod of Ages prints no stats ([[R2280000]]; 1/1 until issue #2280), #120 Wild System's Radiant face gains 2 mana next turn ([[R276]]), Falcon's Radiant face is 8/2, Prototype-X2's 16/16 and xxFreshJack's 12/22 ([[R275]]), and the Drone Battleship's layers are named "Drone Battleship (… Layer)" so that "the Drone Battleship" names all three ([[R1857001]]). The source's header tags Starship and Dormant Starship (on the Drone Battleship's three layers and the Dormant Starship) are read as keywords, Starship and Dormant ([[§6.1]], [[R1839000]], [[R1852004]]), not as tags, so those rows print "Unit, Token" or "Unit" with the keyword in their text. The set adds four family tags, Drone Core, Weapon, War Doctrine and Swarm ([[§5]], [[R1857000]]), and since #2521 two more, Resource on the six Resources and Faction on the five Faction cards (#95, #159 to #162). Stinkyscape: 41 Common, 51 Rare, 43 Epic, 31 Legendary (the Dormant Starship among them), 12 Mythic (178); 42, 50, 43, 29 and 12 (176) until #2521, the owner's first balance patch for the set, made #137 Starfleet Commander Legendary and added the Legendary #176 and the Rare #177. The set was held by [[R1420]]'s gate until its release (issue #1884, [[R1857000]]), which ships it; the AI's own random decks still hold none of it behind [[R1390]]'s soft gate until it is measured on the set (issue #1885). It is built in the catalog card by card, part by part (BUILD M12, S01 to S26, issues #1858 to #1883), from the brief `docs/stinkyscape-set.md`, whose proposed readings (ST-S13-1 and the like) take R numbers from each part's issue block as the part that proves them lands; until then a card's script is a stub. Its cards are written in the terms of the mechanics that landed before it (the brief's ST3): Dormant, Starship Pieces and the launch, Tradable, Resources and Credits, the weapon kit, layered summons and the lethal guard for Units, staged Quests, the two new endings, the AI at the wheel, hidden Nerfs and trade offers, the card metrics, the starscape map and the spell, combat and card-on-card verbs. Two mechanics landed after the rows were first written, #1853's Marketplace ([[R1853000]] to [[R1853005]]) and #1855's board swap and Anomaly boards ([[R1855000]] to [[R1855003]]), and the rows that use them name them and their rulings. In this section "S #N" is Stinkyscape card N, "M+ #N" a Meditative+ card, "M #N" a Meditative card, and "#N", "C #N" and "C+ #N" are Core, Classic and Classic+ cards as elsewhere. "Tunes:" is as in [[§8.6]].

Wrong: nothing more, the Spell still played. The next turn is its controller's, an extra turn ([[R845]]) when the hit came on their own turn and their own next turn when it came on the opponent's, after any extra turns the opponent is already owed ([[R846]]); during it their Units have Charge (`give_units_keyword_next_turn`, the War Doctrines' `unitsHaveKeyword` player modifier with expiry `nextTurnOf`, read beside the auras' keywords, [[§10.4]], [[R1859007]], gone at its cleanup), and a delayed step at its end-of-turn delayed effects, after its end-of-turn triggers and trap window ([[§2.2]]), makes the opponent win the game (`winGame`, [[R850]]) ([[R1870004]]). Then every card in its caster's hand and library whose definition belongs to another faction and not to the chosen one is exiled ([[§6.3]] Exile). A faction is its Faction card and the cards it adds: Faction: ETHICS (S #159) with S #110, #111, #109, #20, #37 and #6; Faction: Vanguard Coalition (S #95) with S #115, #96, #6, #103, #87 and #88; Faction: Golden Accord (S #160) with S #72, #73, #74 and #69; Faction: Pegasus (S #161) with S #113, #94, #123 and #114; Faction: Star-Jumper Core (S #162) with S #75, #76, #78, #79 and #80. So S #6 Tylannt, of ETHICS and Vanguard, stays when either is chosen; a card of no faction is never exiled; the Faction card just added stays; the field, the graveyards and the opponent's zones are untouched ([[R2521004]]). Until #2521, at (1), it exiled every card its caster owned of the four other bundles' definitions from their hand, deck and graveyard ([[R1880001]]). Declares no numbers. |

### 8.11 Training Grounds

Sixty cards, `traininggrounds-001` to `traininggrounds-060`, and one token, from the designer's list of 2026-10-28 (tracker #2193, `JackiOh_TrainingGrounds_Cards.md`) and the brief that reads it (`docs/training-grounds-set.md`, "TG #N" Training Grounds card N): 61 rows. The set was held by [[R1420]]'s gate until its release (issue #2204, [[R2194000]]), which ships it as a patch of the v0.5 line ([[R2204000]]). #1–#40 are the designer's, rarity by number: #1–#15 Common, #16–#27 Rare, #28–#36 Epic, #37–#39 Legendary, #40 Mythic. #39.1 Training-Grounds Golem is the token #39 defines (`traininggrounds-039-1`), `rarity: "Token"` with the designer's Legendary as `printedRarity`, shown in parentheses. #41–#60 are the Legacy cards, snapshots of twenty Core cards: separate entries with their Core cards' data and scripts as they stand when the foundation lands, frozen against later Core patches, at their Core rarities, none Legendary or Mythic; part 2 (#2195) added them ([[R2195003]]). Since issue #2511 each is named `Legacy: ` and its Core card's name and tagged Legacy beside its Core card's tags ([[R2511000]]), and each is its own card: a deck may hold it beside its Core card and a Discover may offer both ([[R2511001]], superseding [[R2195000]] and [[R2195001]]). The set: 26 Common, 19 Rare, 11 Epic, 3 Legendary and 1 Mythic. The designer's types and tags stand, #28 Occidental and #29 Oriental read as Units (each prints stats); the designer's casing and hyphens in names stand; a card's "Training-grounds" or "Tutorial-Grounds" is the set, written Training Grounds ([[R2194001]], [[R2194002]]). Each row's Engine cell gives the brief's reading and the part that builds the card; TG5 has each card's full reading and BUILD M13 its must-pass cases.

The entries (`subsystems/call_to_chaos_training_grounds.rs`): (1) three base Rush Tokens (§7) summoned ([[R64]]), then Taunt and Divine Shield granted to every Unit card of its caster's on the field, in hand and in the deck, the tokens included, a hidden card changed silently ([[R440]], [[R2200001]]); (2) TG #40 Goetzmann Game Designs added until the hand is full, then [[§6.3]] Refresh 4, never past max mana and nothing at or above it ([[R364]], [[R2200001]]); (3) four picks, repeats allowed ([[R60]]), of the set's non-token cards (a pool that names the set, so it worked while the set was held, never this card, [[R387]], [[R2194001]], [[R2200000]]) added at `costOverride` 0, a full hand burning the rest ([[§2.4]]); (4) 8 temporary mana, which may pass max ([[§2.3]]), then 2 draws; (5) six picks of the set's non-token Units, Field Spells, Traps and Field Traps, each summoned by its type, a Trap face-down, with no Cry and no Tribute ([[R1]]), drawn only when its zone is open ([[R129]], [[R2200000]]); (6) as (3), two cards, added Radiant; (7) five base Rush Tokens, then three rest-of-game `unitsHaveKeyword` modifiers on its caster: their Units have Rush, Trample and Pierce, those that arrive later too, kept when the card is gone ([[R1859007]], [[R2200003]]); (8) a base Training-Grounds Golem summoned; (9) one random Call to Chaos of the pool that names no set (the sets that ship, [[R380]]; this edition among them since its set shipped with issue #2204, [[R387]]) added at `costOverride` 0 now, then a rest-of-game start-of-turn effect of its caster's that adds one more at the start of every second turn of theirs, extra turns counted; at most `CHAOS_TG_ALTERNATE_CAP` (3) a player, a roll at the cap resolving into nothing, the add now included ([[R2200002]]); (10) a random Call to Chaos cast, a base form, the chain held by `CALL_TO_CHAOS_CHAIN_CAP` ([[R28]], [[R87]]). The pool of "a random Call to Chaos" (every edition's recast) takes this card since its set shipped, and S #4.6 The Big Crunch's list took it with the release, issue #2204 ([[R2200004]]). Declares no numbers: its outcomes are the engine's table (`ENGINE`, [[R1748003]]). |
